# Hand-check pack: six Qwen3.5-9B headline numbers

Generated by `uv run python scripts/handcheck_pack.py` at commit 0198f6c6; do not hand-edit (rerun the script). Qwen3.5-9B, layer 12. Inputs: the runs' per-item files only (cases.jsonl, outcomes.jsonl), read with the review dashboard's loaders (src/bridge_jspace/review/, docs/review_views.yaml); no verdict or aggregate file is read. Each recount is compared with the `numbers` rows of data/figures/depth/data.csv at 2 decimals (the value and its CI). Tokens are decoded with tokenizer.json of Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B at revision c202236235762e1c871ad0ccb60c8ee5ba337b9a (src/bridge_jspace/pins.py). Status: UNVERIFIED until a human adds it to VERIFIED.md.

**How to recount by hand.** Each section gives the recount first: every k is the number of items whose stored outcome (outcomes.jsonl) is switched, or has the route or class named, out of the n items in its 'every item' list, and the headline is computed from those counts with the formula written out; tally a column of that list to recount a k. Then come 10 items drawn with `random.Random(0).sample(items, 10)` over the recount's items sorted by scene. For each, read the original prompt (decoded from the stored token ids exactly as the model read it; ⟦ ⟧ marks the token where the change is added; ␣ is the space token the read step appends, after which the answer token is read), work out the expected answers from the text yourself (lookup: the locker digit of the asked person's pick; recall: the asked pick's fact from memory; routes and writes: the digit each route or slot predicts), then check each arm's decoded answer token against them and against the stored label. A stored label that disagrees with the decoded token or with your reading is a finding: note the scene.

## 1. The main-test gap (recall extra loss minus lookup extra loss)

**Claim.** On the 9B's main test (two people swap picks), removing the J++ part costs the recall more switches than the lookup, against a random part of the same share: gap 0.53 [0.42, 0.61].

**Key:** `contrast_9b` · **source run:** `results/x0_necessity/qwen35_9b_x0_necessity` · **n:** 200 items

### Recount

| reader | n | S_whole | S_no_part (without J++) | S_no_rand (without its random part) | 1 − S_no_part / S_whole | 1 − S_no_rand / S_whole | extra loss [CI] |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| lookup | 200 | 105/200 = 0.525 | 82/200 = 0.410 | 79/200 = 0.395 | 0.2190 | 0.2476 | −0.03 [−0.08, 0.03] |
| recall | 200 | 197/200 = 0.985 | 92/200 = 0.460 | 190/200 = 0.950 | 0.5330 | 0.0355 | 0.50 [0.41, 0.57] |
| gap (recall − lookup) |  |  |  |  |  |  | **0.53 [0.42, 0.61]** |

- Extra loss per reader: lookup: (1 − 82/105) − (1 − 79/105) = (79 − 82) / 105 = −0.0286; recall: (1 − 92/197) − (1 − 190/197) = (190 − 92) / 197 = 0.4975; gap = 0.4975 − (−0.0286) = 0.5260 → 0.53.
- Every S counts all n items, also those the whole change did not switch (an arm can switch an item the whole change left alone). CI: cluster bootstrap over the original bridge entity, 10,000 resamples, seed 0, both readers on the same draws (the runs' own X.resampled_rates).
- Cross-check: the stored `switched` equals (stored role = b) on 1600 of 1600 stored rows.

**Matches data.csv: yes**

### 10 random items

`random.Random(0).sample(items, 10)` over the 200 items sorted by scene.

#### 1.1 · scene P2-L0-0142 · bridge nitrogen (changed: oxygen)

Original prompt, lookup (the model reads this; ⟦ ⟧ = token 27, where the change is added):


## 2. Sentence order: lookups switched, locker sentence before vs after the asked pick

**Claim.** On the 9B's main test the whole change switches 86 of 100 lookups where the original bridge's locker sentence comes before the asked person's pick sentence, and 19 of 100 where it comes after.

**Key:** `lk_order_before_9b_k` / `lk_order_after_9b_k` · **source run:** `results/x0_necessity/qwen35_9b_x0_necessity` · **n:** 200 items

### Recount

| original bridge's locker sentence vs the asked pick | n | lookups switched by the whole change | share |
|---|---|---|---|
| before | 100 | 86 | 86% |
| after | 100 | 19 | 19% |

- Split: scripts/x0_strata.py features 'org locker vs asked pick' (the keys' source, x0_details); 0 scenes in neither group. Cross-check: the review dashboard's own split (items.sentence_order) agrees on 200 of 200 scenes.

**Matches data.csv: yes**

| key | data.csv | recount | matches |
|---|---|---|---|
| `lk_order_before_9b_k` | 86 | 86 | yes |
| `lk_order_before_9b_n` | 100 | 100 | yes |
| `lk_order_before_9b` | 86% | 86% | yes |
| `lk_order_after_9b_k` | 19 | 19 | yes |
| `lk_order_after_9b_n` | 100 | 100 | yes |
| `lk_order_after_9b` | 19% | 19% | yes |

<details><summary>Every item (n = 200)</summary>

| scene | order | locker sentence / asked pick (sentence no.) | lookup whole: role | switched |

**Claim.** On the 9B, when another item moved, the lookup follows the moved item's identity 0.48, its locker sentence's position 0.51, and never the new value (0.00).

**Key:** `rt_C2_whole_identity_9b` / `rt_C2_whole_position_9b` / `rt_C2_whole_value_9b` · **source run:** `results/x6_routes/qwen35_9b_x6_routes` · **n:** 300 items

| arm | identity | position | value | original | other | position share [CI] | key |

| without the pointer directions (no_alf) | 109/300 | 25/300 | 0/300 | 166/300 | 0/300 | 0.08 [0.05, 0.12] | rt_C2_no_alf_position_9b |

## 6. The locker-first gap (T0.2)

**Claim.** On the 9B's main test, restricted to the scenes whose original bridge's locker sentence comes before the asked pick (locker-first), the gap is 0.34 [0.23, 0.43].

**Key:** `sbo_swap_before_gap_9b` · **source run:** `results/x0_necessity/qwen35_9b_x0_necessity` · **n:** 100 items

### Recount

| reader | n | S_whole | S_no_part (without J++) | S_no_rand (without its random part) | 1 − S_no_part / S_whole | 1 − S_no_rand / S_whole | extra loss [CI] |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| lookup | 100 | 86/100 = 0.860 | 72/100 = 0.720 | 73/100 = 0.730 | 0.1628 | 0.1512 | 0.01 [−0.04, 0.07] |
| recall | 100 | 100/100 = 1.000 | 62/100 = 0.620 | 97/100 = 0.970 | 0.3800 | 0.0300 | 0.35 [0.26, 0.43] |
| gap (recall − lookup) |  |  |  |  |  |  | **0.34 [0.23, 0.43]** |

- Items: the main test's scenes whose x0_strata 'org locker vs asked pick' is before (the split of scripts/x0_split_by_order.py); the same arms and formula as number 1.
- Extra loss per reader: lookup: (1 − 72/86) − (1 − 73/86) = (73 − 72) / 86 = 0.0116; recall: (1 − 62/100) − (1 − 97/100) = (97 − 62) / 100 = 0.3500; gap = 0.3500 − (0.0116) = 0.3384 → 0.34.
- Every S counts all n items, also those the whole change did not switch (an arm can switch an item the whole change left alone). CI: cluster bootstrap over the original bridge entity, 10,000 resamples, seed 0, both readers on the same draws (the runs' own X.resampled_rates).
- Cross-check: the stored `switched` equals (stored role = b) on 800 of 800 stored rows.

**Matches data.csv: yes**

| key | data.csv | recount | matches |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sbo_swap_before_gap_9b` | 0.34 [0.23, 0.43] | 0.34 [0.23, 0.43] | yes |
| `sbo_swap_before_lk_9b` | 0.01 [−0.04, 0.07] | 0.01 [−0.04, 0.07] | yes |
| `sbo_swap_before_rc_9b` | 0.35 [0.26, 0.43] | 0.35 [0.26, 0.43] | yes |
| `sbo_swap_before_n_9b` | 100 | 100 | yes |
| `sbo_swap_before_lksw_9b` | 86% | 86% | yes |

### B · 9B. 45b on the 9B: the bridge word (a, b_half) vs the locker sentences (c, d)

**What.** On the main test's items whose original bridge's locker sentence comes after the asked pick: (a) the bridge-word change at L, (b_half) at L/2, (c) S's states patched into O at every locker-sentence token at L, (d) the same at L/2; S read on S. Lookup switch rates, and c / d as a share of S's switches.

**Model:** Qwen3.5-9B · **run:** `results/x45b_binding/qwen35_9b_x45b_binding` · **compared with:** `results/x45b_binding/qwen35_9b_x45b_binding/verdict-20261026T214250Z/verdict.json` · **items:** all 100 items of the run (cases.jsonl)

#### Recount

| arm | layer | lookups switched: k of n, share | verdict.json | matches |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| none | – | 1 of 100, 0.01 | 1 of 100, 0.01 | yes |
| a | 12 | 20 of 100, 0.20 | 20 of 100, 0.20 | yes |
| b_half | 6 | 68 of 100, 0.68 | 68 of 100, 0.68 | yes |
| c | 12 | 66 of 100, 0.66 | 66 of 100, 0.66 | yes |
| d | 6 | 12 of 100, 0.12 | 12 of 100, 0.12 | yes |
| S | – | 100 of 100, 1.00 | 100 of 100, 1.00 | yes |
| c among S's switches | 12 | 66 of 100, 0.66 | 66 of 100, 0.66 | yes |
| d among S's switches | 6 | 12 of 100, 0.12 | 12 of 100, 0.12 | yes |

- Cross-checks over all 700 lookup rows (7 arms, b_quarter included): the decoded top token equals the stored top_text on 700; the role re-derived from the top token (S's answer's token = b, O's = A) equals the stored role on 700; switched equals (role = b) on 700.
- Switch rate = k / n over all 100 items; c / d among S's switches = (items both S and the arm switch) / (items S switches). Rule (fixed in advance, scripts/x45b_binding.py): holds if c or d switches at least half of S's switches while a stays at or below 0.25.

### B · 27B. 45b on the 27B: the bridge word (a, b_half) vs the locker sentences (c, d)

**What.** On the main test's items whose original bridge's locker sentence comes after the asked pick: (a) the bridge-word change at L, (b_half) at L/2, (c) S's states patched into O at every locker-sentence token at L, (d) the same at L/2; S read on S. Lookup switch rates, and c / d as a share of S's switches.

**Model:** Qwen3.6-27B · **run:** `results/x45b_binding/x45b_binding_27b` · **compared with:** `results/x45b_binding/x45b_binding_27b/verdict-20261026T214250Z/verdict.json` · **items:** all 99 items of the run (cases.jsonl)

#### Recount

| arm | layer | lookups switched: k of n, share | verdict.json | matches |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| none | – | 0 of 99, 0.00 | 0 of 99, 0.00 | yes |
| a | 32 | 8 of 99, 0.08 | 8 of 99, 0.08 | yes |
| b_half | 16 | 78 of 99, 0.79 | 78 of 99, 0.79 | yes |
| c | 32 | 59 of 99, 0.60 | 59 of 99, 0.60 | yes |
| d | 16 | 3 of 99, 0.03 | 3 of 99, 0.03 | yes |
| S | – | 99 of 99, 1.00 | 99 of 99, 1.00 | yes |
| c among S's switches | 32 | 59 of 99, 0.60 | 59 of 99, 0.60 | yes |
| d among S's switches | 16 | 3 of 99, 0.03 | 3 of 99, 0.03 | yes |

- Cross-checks over all 693 lookup rows (7 arms, b_quarter included): the decoded top token equals the stored top_text on 693; the role re-derived from the top token (S's answer's token = b, O's = A) equals the stored role on 693; switched equals (role = b) on 693.
- Switch rate = k / n over all 99 items; c / d among S's switches = (items both S and the arm switch) / (items S switches). Rule (fixed in advance, scripts/x45b_binding.py): holds if c or d switches at least half of S's switches while a stays at or below 0.25.

### C. 45d on Muse: the refit removal in the table format

**What.** Removing the refit basis (rank 4, fit on the other half of the varied scenes) costs the lookup in the table format: extra loss 0.47 [0.34, 0.60] in the verdict.

**Model:** Muse Glimmer 30B · **run:** `results/x0b_generalize/muse30b_x45d` · **compared with:** `results/x0b_generalize/muse30b_x45d/x45d-20261026T202027Z/verdict.json` · **items:** the 238 table-format items of the run (held-out half), the lookup

#### Recount

| arm | lookups switched: k of n, share | verdict.json (table, pcf, lookup) | matches |
|---|---|---|---|
| whole | 79 of 238, 0.33 | switched_whole 79 of 238 | yes |
| without the refit basis (pcf no_part) | 46 of 238, 0.19 | S_no_part 0.1933 × 238 = 46 | yes |
| without its random part (pcf no_rand) | 83 of 238, 0.35 | S_no_rand 0.3487 × 238 = 83 | yes |
| extra loss | (83 − 46) / 79 = 0.47 [0.34, 0.60] | 0.47 [0.34, 0.60], 79 switched of 238 | yes |

- Extra loss = (switches lost without the basis − lost without its random part) / whole switches = (1 − 46/79) − (1 − 83/79) = (83 − 46) / 79 = 0.4684. CI: cluster bootstrap over the bridge item, 10,000 resamples, seed 0 (the run's X.resampled_rates).
- Cross-checks over all 952 lookup rows (4 arms): the role re-derived from the decoded top token equals the stored role on 952; switched equals (role = b) on 952.
- Items: the held-out half (per format, the bridge items not used to fit the basis) as the run built it; layer 26. Rule fixed in advance (batch45.yaml, 45d); the 30-switch minimum per format was decided after the first 45d results (Dan 26 Oct ~21:20) and does not touch this cell.

**Matches the file: yes**

| number | recount | file | matches |
|---|---|---|---|
| n (table-format items) | 238 | 238 | yes |
| whole: switched | 79 of 238, 0.33 | 79 of 238 | yes |
| without the refit basis (pcf no_part): switched | 46 of 238, 0.19 | S_no_part 0.1933 × 238 = 46 | yes |
| without its random part (pcf no_rand): switched | 83 of 238, 0.35 | S_no_rand 0.3487 × 238 = 83 | yes |
| extra loss (the rule's number) | (83 − 46) / 79 = 0.47 [0.34, 0.60] | 0.47 [0.34, 0.60], 79 switched of 238 | yes |

## 45m, 45q and 45p (UNVERIFIED)

Added for Dan's hand-check (27 Oct ~14:40). UNVERIFIED. Each test's rule counts the 9B, the 27B and Muse, so each model is its own part: 3 tests × 3 models, 10 items each, drawn with `random.Random(0).sample(items, 10)` over the run's items sorted by scene (the six numbers' draw). Each part loads its run's per-item files (cases.jsonl, outcomes.jsonl) itself; its recount is compared with the verdict file that predictions/2026-10-26_dan.md cites (Q45.10, Q45.12, Q45.13): counts exactly, shares, extra losses and their CIs to 1e-9. Prompts are decoded from the stored token ids with each run's own model's tokenizer at its pinned revision, exactly as the model reads them (chat template included; ␣ the space token the read appends, after which the answer token is read). In all three tests O and S differ only at the bridge token, so S is given as that one token's change. Marks, as the 45p verify pack (results/x45p/x45p-20261027T012017Z/examples.md): ⟦bridge:…⟧ the bridge word; ⟦locker:…⟧ each locker sentence's tokens (45q); ⟦new:…⟧ the new item's token(s) in its locker sentence and ⟦control:…⟧ the control item's (45p); a marked token's leading space stays outside its mark, and a mark closes after its last whole token (where a sentence's final '.' shares a token with the newline, as Muse's '.\n', the ⟧ starts the next line). The marks are the run's stored positions (cases.jsonl); each is re-derived from the decoded tokens (the tokens where O and S differ; the 'The locker of … is ….' sentences and the items' names found in the decoded text) and compared, for every item of every run: the result is on each card and counted in each part's checks. Each arm's answer is the run's stored top token (outcomes.jsonl), decoded; nothing is recomputed, and an answer the files do not hold would show as 'not stored'.

### 45m · 9B. 45m on the 9B: the clean one-pick design (Q45.10)

**What.** One locker item that nobody picks is added to both prompts and S's asked pick changes from the bridge to it, so only the bridge token differs between O and S. The change (h_S − h_O at the bridge word at the layer fixed in advance) whole, without its J++ part (k = 25, signed), and without a random part of the same share; both readers. The rule reads the locker-before scenes.

**Model:** Qwen3.5-9B · **run:** `results/x0_necessity/qwen35_9b_x45m_fresh` · **compared with:** `results/x0_necessity/x45m-20261026T230628Z/x45m.json` · **items:** the 417 scenes with outcomes at layer 12 (the run's eligible scenes)

#### Recount

| scenes | n | lookup switched: whole / without J++ / without its random part | recall switched: whole / without J++ / without its random part | lookup extra loss [CI] | recall extra loss [CI] | gap [CI] | x45m.json gap | matches |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| locker-before | 151 | 150 / 150 / 148 | 149 / 114 / 146 | −0.01 [−0.03, 0.00] | 0.21 [0.13, 0.29] | 0.23 [0.14, 0.31] | 0.23 [0.14, 0.31] | yes |
| locker-after | 166 | 9 / 3 / 2 | 162 / 27 / 155 | −0.11 [−0.31, 0.00] | 0.79 [0.70, 0.88] | 0.90 [0.71, 1.13] | 0.90 [0.71, 1.13] | yes |
| all | 417 | 216 / 196 / 197 | 411 / 202 / 401 | 0.00 [−0.02, 0.03] | 0.48 [0.42, 0.55] | 0.48 [0.40, 0.55] | 0.48 [0.40, 0.55] | yes |

- Extra loss per reader = (1 − k_no_part / k_whole) − (1 − k_no_rand / k_whole) = (k_no_rand − k_no_part) / k_whole (X0's Jdep − Rdep); gap = recall's − lookup's. CI: cluster bootstrap over the bridge item, 10,000 resamples, seed 0, both readers on the same draws (X.resampled_rates, as the run's verdict).
- Groups (x0_strata's features, as scripts/x45m.py): locker-before 151, locker-after 166, mixed 100 (mixed only in 'all'). Rule (fixed in advance): in locker-before scenes the gap at least 0.3 and the lookup's extra loss at most 0.10, on at least 2 of 3 models.
- Cross-checks over all 3336 rows (both readers, 4 arms): the role re-derived from the decoded top token (S's answer's token = b, O's = A) equals the stored role on 3336; switched equals (role = b) on 3336.
- Stored answers: all 3336 (item, reader, arm) rows are in outcomes.jsonl with a top token; none is recomputed.
- Positions (the marked bridge token is the only token where O and S differ, for both readers, and decodes to the bridge in O and to the new item in S): agree on 417 of 417 items.

### 45m · 27B. 45m on the 27B: the clean one-pick design (Q45.10)

**What.** One locker item that nobody picks is added to both prompts and S's asked pick changes from the bridge to it, so only the bridge token differs between O and S. The change (h_S − h_O at the bridge word at the layer fixed in advance) whole, without its J++ part (k = 25, signed), and without a random part of the same share; both readers. The rule reads the locker-before scenes.

**Model:** Qwen3.6-27B · **run:** `results/x0_necessity/x45m_fresh_27b` · **compared with:** `results/x0_necessity/x45m-20261026T230628Z/x45m.json` · **items:** the 574 scenes with outcomes at layer 32 (the run's eligible scenes)

#### Recount

| scenes | n | lookup switched: whole / without J++ / without its random part | recall switched: whole / without J++ / without its random part | lookup extra loss [CI] | recall extra loss [CI] | gap [CI] | x45m.json gap | matches |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| locker-before | 202 | 197 / 196 / 197 | 162 / 69 / 156 | 0.01 [0.00, 0.02] | 0.54 [0.43, 0.63] | 0.53 [0.42, 0.62] | 0.53 [0.42, 0.62] | yes |
| locker-after | 236 | 0 / 0 / 0 | 190 / 9 / 175 | undefined (the whole change switches none) | 0.87 [0.81, 0.93] | undefined (the whole change switches none) | undefined (the whole change switches none) | yes |
| all | 574 | 222 / 215 / 225 | 445 / 103 / 431 | 0.05 [0.01, 0.08] | 0.74 [0.67, 0.79] | 0.69 [0.63, 0.75] | 0.69 [0.63, 0.75] | yes |

- Extra loss per reader = (1 − k_no_part / k_whole) − (1 − k_no_rand / k_whole) = (k_no_rand − k_no_part) / k_whole (X0's Jdep − Rdep); gap = recall's − lookup's. CI: cluster bootstrap over the bridge item, 10,000 resamples, seed 0, both readers on the same draws (X.resampled_rates, as the run's verdict).
- Groups (x0_strata's features, as scripts/x45m.py): locker-before 202, locker-after 236, mixed 136 (mixed only in 'all'). Rule (fixed in advance): in locker-before scenes the gap at least 0.3 and the lookup's extra loss at most 0.10, on at least 2 of 3 models.
- Cross-checks over all 4592 rows (both readers, 4 arms): the role re-derived from the decoded top token (S's answer's token = b, O's = A) equals the stored role on 4592; switched equals (role = b) on 4592.
- Stored answers: all 4592 (item, reader, arm) rows are in outcomes.jsonl with a top token; none is recomputed.
- Positions (the marked bridge token is the only token where O and S differ, for both readers, and decodes to the bridge in O and to the new item in S): agree on 574 of 574 items.

**Matches the file: yes**

| number | recount | file | matches |
|---|---|---|---|
| locker-before: n and switched counts (both readers, three arms) | n 202 | n 202, switched_whole and S_no_part / S_no_rand × n | yes |
| locker-before: lookup's extra loss | 0.01 [0.00, 0.02] | 0.01 [0.00, 0.02] | yes |

### 45m · Muse. 45m on the Muse: the clean one-pick design (Q45.10)

**What.** One locker item that nobody picks is added to both prompts and S's asked pick changes from the bridge to it, so only the bridge token differs between O and S. The change (h_S − h_O at the bridge word at the layer fixed in advance) whole, without its J++ part (k = 25, signed), and without a random part of the same share; both readers. The rule reads the locker-before scenes.

**Model:** Muse Glimmer 30B · **run:** `results/x0_necessity/muse30b_x45m_fresh` · **compared with:** `results/x0_necessity/x45m-20261026T230628Z/x45m.json` · **items:** the 409 scenes with outcomes at layer 26 (the run's eligible scenes)

#### Recount

| scenes | n | lookup switched: whole / without J++ / without its random part | recall switched: whole / without J++ / without its random part | lookup extra loss [CI] | recall extra loss [CI] | gap [CI] | x45m.json gap | matches |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| locker-before | 143 | 124 / 124 / 130 | 84 / 47 / 83 | 0.05 [0.01, 0.10] | 0.43 [0.28, 0.60] | 0.38 [0.23, 0.54] | 0.38 [0.23, 0.54] | yes |
| locker-after | 170 | 0 / 0 / 0 | 138 / 12 / 129 | undefined (the whole change switches none) | 0.85 [0.73, 0.93] | undefined (the whole change switches none) | undefined (the whole change switches none) | yes |
| all | 409 | 131 / 132 / 140 | 293 / 82 / 288 | 0.06 [0.02, 0.11] | 0.70 [0.60, 0.79] | 0.64 [0.52, 0.73] | 0.64 [0.52, 0.73] | yes |

- Extra loss per reader = (1 − k_no_part / k_whole) − (1 − k_no_rand / k_whole) = (k_no_rand − k_no_part) / k_whole (X0's Jdep − Rdep); gap = recall's − lookup's. CI: cluster bootstrap over the bridge item, 10,000 resamples, seed 0, both readers on the same draws (X.resampled_rates, as the run's verdict).
- Groups (x0_strata's features, as scripts/x45m.py): locker-before 143, locker-after 170, mixed 96 (mixed only in 'all'). Rule (fixed in advance): in locker-before scenes the gap at least 0.3 and the lookup's extra loss at most 0.10, on at least 2 of 3 models.
- Cross-checks over all 3272 rows (both readers, 4 arms): the role re-derived from the decoded top token (S's answer's token = b, O's = A) equals the stored role on 3272; switched equals (role = b) on 3272.
- Stored answers: all 3272 (item, reader, arm) rows are in outcomes.jsonl with a top token; none is recomputed.
- Positions (the marked bridge token is the only token where O and S differ, for both readers, and decodes to the bridge in O and to the new item in S): agree on 409 of 409 items.

**Matches the file: yes**

| number | recount | file | matches |
|---|---|---|---|

Rule (45b's, fixed in advance): (c) or (d) switches at least half of the lookups S switches while (a) stays at or below 0.25, on at least 2 of 3 models. CI: cluster bootstrap over the bridge item, 10,000 resamples, seed 0 (X.resampled_rates; c / d among S's switches = the ratio of the resampled shares, as the run's verdict).

- Stored answers: all 3618 (item, reader, arm) rows are in outcomes.jsonl with a top token; none is recomputed.

Rule (45b's, fixed in advance): (c) or (d) switches at least half of the lookups S switches while (a) stays at or below 0.25, on at least 2 of 3 models. The link arms (c_nojpp, c_norand) are reported beside it (link.json), not in it. CI: cluster bootstrap over the bridge item, 10,000 resamples, seed 0 (X.resampled_rates; c / d among S's switches = the ratio of the resampled shares, as the run's verdict).
- Cross-checks over all 5184 rows (both readers, 9 arms): the decoded top token equals the stored top_text on 5184; the role re-derived from the top token equals the stored role on 5184; switched equals (role = b) on 5184.
- Stored answers: all 5184 (item, reader, arm) rows are in outcomes.jsonl with a top token; none is recomputed.
- Positions (the marked bridge token is the only token where O and S differ, for both readers; the marked locker tokens are those of every 'The locker of … is ….' sentence in the decoded prompt, equal to the stored lockers, patched and source positions and character marks): agree on 288 of 288 items.

Rule (45b's, fixed in advance): (c) or (d) switches at least half of the lookups S switches while (a) stays at or below 0.25, on at least 2 of 3 models. The link arms (c_nojpp, c_norand) are reported beside it (link.json), not in it. CI: cluster bootstrap over the bridge item, 10,000 resamples, seed 0 (X.resampled_rates; c / d among S's switches = the ratio of the resampled shares, as the run's verdict).
- Cross-checks over all 3690 rows (both readers, 9 arms): the decoded top token equals the stored top_text on 3690; the role re-derived from the top token equals the stored role on 3690; switched equals (role = b) on 3690.
- Stored answers: all 3690 (item, reader, arm) rows are in outcomes.jsonl with a top token; none is recomputed.
- Positions (the marked bridge token is the only token where O and S differ, for both readers; the marked locker tokens are those of every 'The locker of … is ….' sentence in the decoded prompt, equal to the stored lockers, patched and source positions and character marks): agree on 205 of 205 items.

### 45p · 27B. 45p on the 27B: the new item's earlier mention, its locker first (Q45.13)

**What.** On 45m's scenes whose new item's locker sentence comes before the asked pick: (1) the base edit (the bridge-word change without its J++ part), then on top of it the new item's token(s) in its locker sentence without their own J++ part (2), without a random part of the same share (3), or the control item's token(s) without their J++ part (4); all at the layer fixed in advance, on O.

### 45p · Muse. 45p on the Muse: the new item's earlier mention, its locker first (Q45.13)

**What.** On 45m's scenes whose new item's locker sentence comes before the asked pick: (1) the base edit (the bridge-word change without its J++ part), then on top of it the new item's token(s) in its locker sentence without their own J++ part (2), without a random part of the same share (3), or the control item's token(s) without their J++ part (4); all at the layer fixed in advance, on O.
